            ".specify/features",    # feature registry rows (072 line + detail)
            ".specify/memory/exec-quant/records/",   # dogfooding loop records (new entries only)
            ".specify/memory/session/",                # dogfooding loop records (new entries only)

            "tests/contract/", "tests/fixtures/",      # contract tests are the declared surface
            ".claude/commands/", ".github/prompts/",
            ".opencode/command/", ".qoder/commands/",  # per-tool copies (regenerated)
            ".specify/scripts/", ".specify/shared/",  # mirrors
            ".specify/skills/",  # all skill mirrors are produced by sync-mirrors (generated, never hand-edited)
        )
        offenders = []
        for rel in status:

    def test_canonical_engine_does_not_self_resolve_to_the_framework_repo(tmp_path: Path):
        """Run the CANONICAL engine from a workspace that is not this repo.
    
        Before the fix, resolve_root walked up looking for any ancestor containing
        `.specify/` — which the framework repo always does — so every invocation
        resolved here and wrote workspace state into the repo.
        """

        (tmp_path / ".specify" / "memory").mkdir(parents=True)

        import sys
        spec = importlib.util.spec_from_file_location("_engine_hats_check", ENGINE)
        module = importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec)
        sys.modules[spec.name] = module
        spec.loader.exec_module(module)
    
        prev = Path.cwd()
        import os
        os.chdir(tmp_path)
        try:
            resolved = module.resolve_root(None)
        finally:
            os.chdir(prev)

    def test_the_goal_team_sentences_use_the_new_keys_and_the_stage_chain_is_unchanged():
        protocol = _text(PROTOCOL)
        skill = _text(SKILL)
    
        # The goal/team sentences are written in the binding model's vocabulary.
        assert "goal_slug" in protocol, "the goal sentence names the goal by its binding identity"
        assert "`goal_slug`" in skill, "the driver writes the new spine key"
    
        # The retired scope syntax appears nowhere in the driver's protocol or manual.
        for text, name in ((protocol, "checkpoint-protocol.md"), (skill, "SKILL.md")):
            for retired in RETIRED_SCOPE_SYNTAX:
                assert retired not in text, (
                    f"{name} still carries the retired scope syntax {retired!r}")
    
        # The legacy spine keys survive only as a read-only alias statement, never as a
        # write instruction: the driver's write rule names the new keys.
        assert "origin_spec_dir" in skill, "the new spine key set is complete"
        assert "MUST 只落新键" in skill or "writes the new keys only" in skill, (
            "the driver states the new keys are the only write targets")
    
        # Preserved: the stage chain and the completion-sentinel authority.
        assert STAGE_CHAIN in skill, "the stage chain literal is unchanged"
        assert "sentinel" in protocol, "the sentinel remains the completion authority"
        assert "### 5. Direction audit" in protocol, (
            "the direction audit stays a mandatory checkpoint part")
    
        # Preserved: the five parts stay in order and no new part is inserted.
        headings = [line for line in protocol.splitlines() if line.startswith("### ")]
        numbered = headings[:5]
        assert [h.split()[1] for h in numbered] == ["1.", "2.", "3.", "4.", "5."], (
            f"the checkpoint part sequence changed: {numbered}")
    
        # Decomposition §22 is retitled to the binding model, not the retired scope.

    def test_audit_reports_the_affected_feature_with_claim_verdict_and_every_reason():
        """C-12: the list is readable without re-running the checker.
    
        901 is the case's would-become-`Blocked` subject: its claim (`Implemented`) exceeds its
        evidence under the new rules, so the report carries the claimed status, the computed verdict
        and **every** reason — the zero-pass refusal and the claim-exceeds-evidence refusal, both —

    def test_c6_feedback_step_edit_is_additive_single_line():
        proc = _git("diff", "-U0", PRE_CHANGE_HEAD, "--", "shared/workflow/feedback-step.md")
        assert proc.returncode == 0, "git diff failed: %r" % proc.stderr
        removed: list[str] = []
        added: list[str] = []
        for line in proc.stdout.splitlines():
            if line.startswith("---") or line.startswith("+++"):
                continue

            elif line.startswith("+") and line[1:].strip():
                added.append(line[1:])
        declared_removals = {

    def test_c57_shipped_text_adds_no_fresh_neutrality_violation():
        violations: Dict[str, List[str]] = {}
        for root_name in ("templates", "shared", "skills", "agents"):
            for path in (FRAMEWORK_ROOT / root_name).rglob("*"):
                if not path.is_file() or path.suffix not in {".md", ".py", ".sh", ".yaml", ".yml", ".json"}:
                    continue
                rel = path.relative_to(FRAMEWORK_ROOT).as_posix()
                text = path.read_text(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace")
                for pattern in (
                    re.compile(r"\b(?:req|requirement)\s+\d{3}\b", re.IGNORECASE),
                    re.compile(r"\bFeature\s+\d{3}\b"),
                    re.compile(r"\.specify/specs/\d{3}-"),
                    re.compile(r"\.specify/features/\d{3}"),
                ):
                    if pattern.search(text):
                        violations.setdefault(rel, []).append(pattern.pattern)
        fresh = sorted(set(violations) - PRE_EXISTING_NEUTRALITY_DEBT)
>       assert not fresh, (
            "C-57: Feature 072 shipped text introduced fresh framework-runtime "

    def test_c5_wiring_surface_is_closed():
        offenders: list[str] = []
        for path in sorted(COMMANDS_DIR.glob("*.md")):
            if path.name in WIRING_TEMPLATES + POST_077_WIRING_TEMPLATES:
                continue
            text = path.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
            offenders.extend(
                "%s -> %s" % (path.relative_to(REPO_ROOT).as_posix(), token)
                for token in ENGINE_OR_OWNER_TOKENS
                if token in text
            )
        for skill_file in sorted(SKILLS_DIR.glob("external/*/SKILL.md")) + sorted(
            SKILLS_DIR.glob("internal/*/SKILL.md")
        ):
            if skill_file.relative_to(REPO_ROOT).as_posix() in WIRING_SKILLS:
                continue

            offenders.extend(
                "%s -> %s" % (skill_file.relative_to(REPO_ROOT).as_posix(), token)
                for token in ENGINE_OR_OWNER_TOKENS

            )

    def test_audit_writes_nothing(tmp_path):
        """C-11, under the contract's own test name: the audit reports and writes nothing.

        `tmp_path` is a tree an implementation actually could modify — a recursive content hash before
        and after the run fails the moment the audit caches, locks, or rewrites anything under the
        root it reads. The **`git status --porcelain`** pair is C-11's named verification, observed
        over the repository this test runs in: it is attributed to the tree it actually observes (the
        checkout), which is why the copy lives outside it — the fixture and the status read never
        share a root, so neither assertion silently stands in for the other.
        """

            ["git", "status", "--porcelain"], cwd=str(ROOT), capture_output=True, text=True
        ).stdout

    def test_c68_the_entry_set_is_identical_to_a_render_without_the_dimension(tmp_path):

        # back the store-less render byte for byte — every other field, the ordering, the cap and the summary survive.

    def test_a_block_lands_blocked_after_the_record(tmp_path):

        GATE-5 reads this leg: the gate is the default (no `--gate-cmd` given), the fixture blocks,
        the line carries `integration_result.status=failed` with the `closure-blocked` reason, its

_ test_c70_neither_delivered_script_names_a_schema_version_field_slot_or_quality_subkey _
[gw118] linux -- Python 3.11.11 /usr/local/bin/python3

    def test_c70_neither_delivered_script_names_a_schema_version_field_slot_or_quality_subkey():
        """C-70 / C-71: the record's schema is reached by path through the engine; no delivered script re-spells it.
    
        The scan is an AST scan over the **string literals** of the delivered scripts: a literal naming a schema
        version, a ``quality`` sub-key or a closed vocabulary member would be a second definition of the declaration
        owner's fact. Comments and docstrings are excluded (a docstring names the owner's path, which is the legal
        reference form); only executable string literals are judged.
        """
        delivered = [
            Path("scripts") / "python" / "status-digest.py",
            Path("scripts") / "python" / "measure.py",
        ]
        #: The declaration owner's own vocabulary members, read from it rather than re-spelled here (C-71).
        declaration = (REPO_ROOT / "shared" / "declarations" / "facts" / "quant-record-schema.yaml").read_text(
            encoding="utf-8"
        )
        vocabulary = set(re.findall(r"^\s+-\s+([a-z][a-z-]+)\s*$", declaration, re.M))
    
        offenders = []
        for rel in delivered:
            path = REPO_ROOT / rel
            assert path.is_file(), "a delivered script is missing: %s" % rel
            tree = ast.parse(path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"), filename=str(path))
            docstrings = set()
            for node in ast.walk(tree):
                if isinstance(node, (ast.Module, ast.FunctionDef, ast.AsyncFunctionDef, ast.ClassDef)):
                    body = getattr(node, "body", [])
                    if body and isinstance(body[0], ast.Expr) and isinstance(body[0].value, ast.Constant):
                        docstrings.add(id(body[0].value))
            for node in ast.walk(tree):
                if not isinstance(node, ast.Constant) or not isinstance(node.value, str):
                    continue
                if id(node) in docstrings:
                    continue
                text = node.value
                if "schema_version" in text or "quality." in text or "elapsed_ms" in text:
                    offenders.append((rel.as_posix(), text, node.lineno))
                elif text in vocabulary and len(text) > 3:
                    offenders.append((rel.as_posix(), text, node.lineno))
>       assert not offenders, (
            "C-70/C-71: a delivered script re-spells the declaration owner's fact instead of reaching it by path: %r"
            % (offenders,)
        )
E       AssertionError: C-70/C-71: a delivered script re-spells the declaration owner's fact instead of reaching it by path: [('scripts/python/status-digest.py', 'agent', 1116)]
E       assert not [('scripts/python/status-digest.py', 'agent', 1116)]

    def test_no_second_map_or_boundary_table_exists():
        roster = set(_roster_stages())
        assert roster, "the stage roster must parse before carrier uniqueness is judged"
        duplicates = []
        for path in _md_files():
            if path == OWNER:
                continue
            try:
                text = path.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
            except (OSError, UnicodeDecodeError):
                continue
            if "强制状态" not in text and "门禁处理" not in text:
                continue
            for header, rows in _tables(text.splitlines()):
                if "强制状态" in header and "强制门禁" in header:
                    for row in rows:
                        if len(row) >= 6 and row[0].strip("` ") in roster:
                            duplicates.append((str(path.relative_to(REPO_ROOT)), row[0]))
                            break
                if "门禁处理" in header and "边界" in header:
                    ids = {row[0].strip("` ") for row in rows if row}
                    if {"a", "b", "c", "d"} & ids:
                        duplicates.append((str(path.relative_to(REPO_ROOT)), "boundary table"))
                        break
>       assert not duplicates, (
            "a second mapping/boundary carrier exists (contract C-1/C-9): %r" % (duplicates,)
        )

    @pytest.mark.contract
    def test_wording_hit_refuses_the_write(tmp_path, capsys):
        # A registry whose rendered line would carry blocking wording: the sandbox owner
        # gains a lowercase blocking heading, an agent handler anchors on it.
        registry = REGISTRY_TEXT.replace(
            "| h-run-determination | engine | determine | non-blocking | 10 | "
            "scripts/python/run-determination.py |",
            "| h-run-determination | engine | determine | non-blocking | 10 | "
            "scripts/python/run-determination.py |\n"
            "| h-stop-and-confirm | agent | stop and confirm the draft | non-blocking "
            "| 25 | shared/workflow/feedback-step.md |")
        # Anchor at the table header separator: inserting mid-row (substring inside a
        # row carrying a 覆盖说明 cell) would corrupt the row's cells.
        registry = registry.replace(
            "| handler_id | selector | scope | 覆盖说明 |\n"
            "|------------|----------|-------|----------|",
            "| handler_id | selector | scope | 覆盖说明 |\n"
            "|------------|----------|-------|----------|\n"
            "| h-stop-and-confirm | @point:wrap-up | all | wording-gate drill row |")
        ws = CE._build_workspace(tmp_path, registry)
        owner = ws / "shared" / "workflow" / "feedback-step.md"
        owner.write_text(
            owner.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
            + "\n## stop and confirm the draft\n\nDrill heading.\n",
            encoding="utf-8")
        rc = probe_utils.main(["--action", "compile-embeds",
                               "--workspace-root", str(ws)])
        assert rc == 2, "generated wording hitting BLOCKING_PATTERNS must refuse (exit 2)"
        err = capsys.readouterr().err

    def test_skills_use_installed_absolute_shared_form():
        """Sibling skills reference shared docs via the installed-absolute shared path.
    
        Scans every markdown file in a skill, not just SKILL.md: the references/ files ship
        into `.specify/skills/<name>/` too, and a bare `shared/<type>/` path there is just as
        unresolvable in a client project. Scanning only the top-level file left three of them
        undetected while the test was red for an unrelated reason.
        """
        import re
    
        offenders = []
        scanned = 0
        bare = re.compile(r"(?<![\w./])shared/(" + "|".join(SHARED_TYPES) + r")/")
        for f in _iter_files("skills/**/*.md"):
            scanned += 1
            text = f.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
            if bare.search(text):
                offenders.append(str(f.relative_to(ROOT)))
        assert scanned > 100, (
            f"only {scanned} skill markdown files scanned — the glob looks truncated, which "
            f"would let an empty offender list pass without proving anything"
        )
>       assert not offenders, f"skills must use .specify/shared/<type>/ form: {offenders}"

    @pytest.mark.contract
    def test_shipped_surface_has_no_framework_runtime_references():
        violations = []
        for root_name in SHIPPED_ROOTS:
            for path in (REPO_ROOT / root_name).rglob("*"):
                if not path.is_file() or path.suffix not in {".md", ".py", ".sh", ".yaml", ".yml", ".json"}:
                    continue
                rel = path.relative_to(REPO_ROOT).as_posix()
                text = path.read_text(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace")
                for pattern in BANNED_PATTERNS:
                    for m in pattern.finditer(text):
                        line = text[: m.start()].count("\n") + 1
                        snippet = text.splitlines()[line - 1].strip()[:90]
                        violations.append(f"{rel}:{line}: {snippet}")
        fresh = [v for v in violations
                 if v.split(":")[0] not in KNOWN_DEBT]

_____ test_c11_no_dangling_guideline_pointer_on_either_instruction_surface _____
[gw11] linux -- Python 3.11.11 /usr/local/bin/python3

    def test_c11_no_dangling_guideline_pointer_on_either_instruction_surface():
        surfaces = (("template", TEMPLATE), ("live", LIVE))
        pointed: set[str] = set()
        for label, path in surfaces:
            names = set(GUIDELINE_POINTER_RE.findall(_text(path)))
            assert names, f"the {label} instruction surface carries no guideline pointer at all"
            pointed |= names
    
        # A pointer's whole job is to let a reader open the file, so the target that must exist
        # is the one the reader actually resolves: `.specify/shared/guidelines/<name>.md`.
        # Checking only the framework source leaves the downstream failure mode invisible —
        # proven by drill: with the runtime copy moved aside and the source intact, a
        # source-only version of this clause still passed. Both halves are asserted, because
        # the source is what regenerates the runtime copy.
        runtime_dir = ROOT / ".specify" / "shared" / "guidelines"
        dangling_runtime = sorted(n for n in pointed if not (runtime_dir / n).is_file())
        assert not dangling_runtime, (
            "instruction surfaces point at guidelines with no runtime copy under "
            ".specify/shared/guidelines/ — a reader following the pointer finds nothing, which "
            "is the delivery window FR-038 / SC-017 exists to close "
            f"(run scripts/python/sync-mirrors.py --write shared): {dangling_runtime}"
        )
        dangling_source = sorted(n for n in pointed if not (GUIDELINES / n).is_file())
        assert not dangling_source, (
            "instruction surfaces point at guidelines with no framework source under "
            f"shared/guidelines/ — the runtime copy cannot be regenerated: {dangling_source}"
        )
    
        # C-11(b): the remainder must not grow past the three structurally unpointed files.
        # Anti-vacuity: name the three and require them to exist, so an empty guidelines
        # directory cannot make the subset assertion pass on nothing.
        for name in UNPOINTED_BY_STRUCTURE:
            assert (GUIDELINES / name).is_file(), (
                f"C-11(b) names {name} as unpointed-by-structure but it is not in shared/guidelines/"
            )
        full = {p.name for p in GUIDELINES.glob("*.md")}
        remainder = full - pointed
        unexpected = sorted(remainder - set(UNPOINTED_BY_STRUCTURE))
>       assert not unexpected, (
            "a guideline lost its instruction-surface pointer, so it can no longer dangle "
            "loudly — coverage shrank silently. Either restore the pointer or amend "
            f"ambient-section.md C-11(b) with a recorded reason: {unexpected}"
        )
E       AssertionError: a guideline lost its instruction-surface pointer, so it can no longer dangle loudly — coverage shrank silently. Either restore the pointer or amend ambient-section.md C-11(b) with a recorded reason: ['reporting-obligations.md', 'self-explanatory.md']
E       assert not ['reporting-obligations.md', 'self-explanatory.md']

[gw62] linux -- Python 3.11.11 /usr/local/bin/python3

    @pytest.mark.integration
    def test_c019_no_new_command_no_new_tier():
        """The obligation is reachable from existing verification steps only: no new command, no new tier.
    
        "No new command" is read as a DELTA against the base commit rather than as an absolute roster (the roster's
        owner is another contract), which is the only form in which this feature can make the claim honestly.
    
        red-when: this feature added a command template, or a tier row — either would make the check reachable by a
        surface the specification never opened.
        """
        # 2026-11-07: the merged trunk carries Feature 088's two sanctioned command templates
        # (status.md, measure.md). This feature's delta check still rejects every other addition.
        sanctioned_later_commands = {"templates/commands/status.md", "templates/commands/measure.md"}
        added = [
            rel
            for rel in changed_paths("templates/commands")
            if is_new(rel) and rel not in sanctioned_later_commands
        ]
>       assert not added, f"no new command template may be introduced: {added}"
E       AssertionError: no new command template may be introduced: ['templates/commands/seal.md']
E       assert not ['templates/commands/seal.md']

/usr/lib64/python3.11/pathlib.py:1044: FileNotFoundError
____ test_maturity_promotion_does_not_retroactively_rewrite_earlier_states _____
[gw76] linux -- Python 3.11.11 /usr/local/bin/python3

tmp_path = PosixPath('/tmp/pytest-of-agent/pytest-8406/popen-gw76/test_maturity_promotion_does_n0')

    @requires_generator
    def test_maturity_promotion_does_not_retroactively_rewrite_earlier_states(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
        """LC-10 — report-only-era items stay as recorded after an L1 -> L2 promotion.
    
        The property holds because the ledger is append-only (IL-1) and each event
        carries the maturity in force when it happened; a later promotion appends, it
        never mutates. This asserts that the generator honours that rather than
        re-deriving state from the team's *current* maturity.
        """
        (tmp_path / ".specify/teams/mat/runs").mkdir(parents=True)
        (tmp_path / ".specify/teams/mat/team.md").write_text(
            team_md("mat", pattern="continuous"), encoding="utf-8"
        )
        (tmp_path / ".specify/teams/mat/runs/20260710T080000Z-report.md").write_text(
            CONTRACT_REPORT.format(slug="mat", day="10", outcome="completed", gen=1, deliverable="产物"),
            encoding="utf-8",
        )
        ledger = tmp_path / ".specify/teams/mat/items.jsonl"
        ledger.write_text(
            json.dumps(
                {
                    "item_id": "TI-0001",
                    "title": "L1 期间只观测未行动的条目",
                    "phase_ref": "PH-0001",

__________ test_cli_subprocess_smoke_pins_payload_and_exit_semantics ___________
[gw174] linux -- Python 3.11.11 /usr/local/bin/python3

tmp_path = PosixPath('/tmp/pytest-of-agent/pytest-8406/popen-gw174/test_cli_subprocess_smoke_pins0')

    def test_cli_subprocess_smoke_pins_payload_and_exit_semantics(tmp_path: Path):
        """≥1 true CLI subprocess run stays in this file (FR-020).
    
        Pins the engine's CLI contract on a real interpreter subprocess — fixed
        9-key envelope, ok/errors shape, graded exit codes — and asserts the
        in-process runner used by every other test in this file produces the
        *same* payload and exit code for identical argv on a twin workspace. If a
        future engine change makes the two faces diverge, this pin fires.
        """
        ws_cli = tmp_path / "cli"
        ws_ipc = tmp_path / "ipc"
        for d in (ws_cli, ws_ipc):
            (d / ".specify" / "templates").mkdir(parents=True)
            (d / ".specify" / "templates" / "proactive-trigger-seed.json").write_bytes(
                SEED.read_bytes()
            )
            (d / ".specify" / "memory").mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
            subprocess.run(["git", "init", "-q", "."], cwd=d, check=True)
    
        # init: envelope shape + ok flag, pinned on the subprocess face.
        proc_cli = cli_run(["--action", "init"], ws_cli)
        assert proc_cli.returncode == EXIT_OK, proc_cli.stderr
        init_env = json.loads(proc_cli.stdout)
        assert init_env["ok"] is True
        assert init_env["action"] == "init"
        assert init_env["errors"] == [] and init_env["warnings"] == []
        assert set(init_env) == {
            "ok", "action", "workspaceRoot", "generatedAt", "errors",
            "warnings", "semanticJudgmentPending", "notes", "payload",
        }, "the fixed 9-key envelope shape drifted"
    
        # Same argv through the in-process entry point: identical envelope.
        proc_ipc = run(["--action", "init"], ws_ipc)
        assert proc_ipc.returncode == proc_cli.returncode
>       assert normalized_envelope(proc_ipc.stdout) == normalized_envelope(proc_cli.stdout)
E       AssertionError: assert {'action': 'i...k': True, ...} == {'action': 'i...k': True, ...}
E         
E         Omitting 6 identical items, use -vv to show
E         Differing items:
E         {'payload': {'config': {'enabled': True, 'minSample': 5, 'probeBudgetPct': 20, 'telemetryWindow': 200, ...}, 'created': True, 'rules': 13, 'situations': 13}} != {'payload': {'config': {'enabled': True, 'minSample': 5, 'probeBudgetPct': 20, 'telemetryWindow': 3, ...}, 'created': False, 'rules': 13, 'situations': 13}}

___________________ test_sc005_engine_domain_is_single_owner ___________________
[gw43] linux -- Python 3.11.11 /usr/local/bin/python3

    @pytest.mark.contract
    def test_sc005_engine_domain_is_single_owner():
        """This feature added no second enumeration of the engine layers.
    
        The inventory half of `SC-005`, read as a change-scoped inventory: whatever the repository already carries
        inside draw-diagram's own tree belongs to that capability's owner, and the contract's `C-010` example is a
        schema illustration. What matters for this delivery is that no file it touched added one, so the assertion
        reads the changed-file set and pins the verdict on the code this feature authored.
    
        The population and the claim are kept apart on purpose. The **population** is every tracked file under the
        scanned roots — non-empty, so an empty read means the scan broke rather than that the claim held. The
        **claim** is about the subset this feature actually authored: files whose own text (not a test's fixture
        string, not this capability's owner tree) enumerates two or more engine layers. That subset must be empty
        both because no such file exists today and because the assertion has to be able to say so out loud instead
        of failing on a vacuous premise.
    
        red-when: a file this feature changed enumerates two or more engine layers, i.e. it published a second
        engine list.
        """
        names = engine_names()
        population = tracked_files()
        assert population, "the premise: the scanned roots carry tracked files to inventory"
    
        # `docs-utils.py` carries the `.mmd` / `.puml` *file suffixes* a diagram reference may name. A suffix is not
        # an engine layer, and its own obligation block cites the engine names it reads out of a record; neither is a
        # second enumeration. `tests/` is this feature's evidence, not a surface a reader consults to pick an engine.
        allowed_paths = {"skills/external/create-docs/scripts/docs-utils.py"}
        allowed_prefixes = (DRAW_DIAGRAM_TREE, ".specify/skills/draw-diagram/", "tests/")
        authored = [rel for rel in sorted(set(files_changed_by_this_feature(SCANNED_ROOTS))
                                          | set(files_changed_in_worktree(SCANNED_ROOTS)))
                    if rel not in allowed_paths and not rel.startswith(allowed_prefixes)]
>       assert set(authored) <= set(population), "the scan reported files outside the scanned roots: " + str(
            sorted(set(authored) - set(population)))
E       AssertionError: the scan reported files outside the scanned roots: ['skills/internal/study-project/SKILL.md']
E       assert {'scripts/bas...ents.sh', ...} <= {'scripts/bas...ents.sh', ...}
E         
E         Extra items in the left set:
E         'skills/internal/study-project/SKILL.md'

tmp_path = PosixPath('/tmp/pytest-of-agent/pytest-8406/popen-gw14/test_the_resolution_input_erro0')

    def test_the_resolution_input_error_shapes_refuse_without_inventing_a_spec_dir(tmp_path):
        """The four GW-03 shapes are input errors (exit-2 class), each naming its subject.
    
        Per shape, the whole pipeline runs on its own worktree: the resolution refuses, the reason is
        `closure-input-error`, the line keeps its `completed` state and no merge follows. The branches
        below are the shapes the live registry actually exposes (most lines carry a branch with no
        three-digit prefix), which is why a silent skip would have made the gate a no-op for most of
        the fleet.
        """
        shapes = [
            # (name, branch, features rows, expected subject fragment)
            ("branchless", "feature-no-id", [("081", ".specify/specs/081/requirements.md")], "branch"),
            ("no-row", "081-norow", [("999", ".specify/specs/999/requirements.md")], "081"),
            ("dash-cell", "081-dash", [("081", "-")], "Spec Path"),
            ("missing-dir", "081-missing", [("081", ".specify/specs/999-absent/requirements.md")],
             "999-absent"),
        ]
        reg = tmp_path / "reg"
        reg.mkdir()
        for name, branch, rows, subject in shapes:
            repo, wt = _make_line_repo(tmp_path, name, branch,
                                       spec_cell=".specify/specs/unused/requirements.md",
                                       fixture=None, features_rows=rows)
            line_id = _register_line(reg, repo, wt, branch)
            proc, summary = _run_integrator(tmp_path, repo, reg, line_id)
            assert proc.returncode != 0, "%s: an unresolvable spec dir must refuse the merge" % name
            row = _result_for(summary, line_id)
            assert row["reason"] == "closure-input-error", (
                "%s: the shape is an input error, not a block and not a `quality-gate`: %r"
                % (name, row)
            )

        # collection; in a full suite the unit tier runs >300 s later, so an at=NOW

tests/unit/test_loop_state_engine.py:1146: AssertionError
_ test_c43_ordering_puts_active_lines_first_then_descending_activity_with_line_id_ties _
[gw11] linux -- Python 3.11.11 /usr/local/bin/python3

tmp_path = PosixPath('/tmp/pytest-of-agent/pytest-8406/popen-gw11/test_c43_ordering_puts_active_0')

    def test_c43_ordering_puts_active_lines_first_then_descending_activity_with_line_id_ties(tmp_path):
        """Contract C-43: active first, then descending last activity, ties by ``line_id`` ascending."""
        constants = _load_engine_constants()
        active_state = sorted(constants["ACTIVE_STATES"])[0]
        idle_states = sorted(constants["STATE_VALUES"] - constants["ACTIVE_STATES"])
        idle_state = idle_states[0]
        workspace = tmp_path / "workspace"
        workspace.mkdir()
        registry = _registry(tmp_path)
        # Two idle entries share one timestamp, so only the tie-break can order them; the active entry is the oldest of
        # all three and must still come first.
        _write_entry(
            registry,
            _entry("t027-b-oldest-active", workspace, state=active_state,
                   updated_at="2026-11-06T00:00:00Z"),
        )
        _write_entry(
            registry,
            _entry("t027-z-tie", workspace, state=idle_state, updated_at="2026-11-06T05:00:00Z"),
        )
        _write_entry(
            registry,
            _entry("t027-a-tie", workspace, state=idle_state, updated_at="2026-11-06T05:00:00Z"),
        )
    
        proc = _digest(workspace, registry, "--cap", "3")
    
        order = [
            ln.split("line_id=", 1)[1].split(" ", 1)[0]
            for ln in proc.stdout.splitlines()
            if ln.startswith("line_id=")
        ]
>       assert order == ["t027-b-oldest-active", "t027-a-tie", "t027-z-tie"], (
            "the declared total ordering is active lines first, then descending last activity, ties by line_id"

    def test_c44_an_entry_older_than_the_stale_bound_is_marked_stale_with_its_age(tmp_path):

    def test_sc003_reading_line_is_recomputable_from_its_own_fields():
        """The enclosed count argument must close arithmetically on the printed fields.
    
        ``complement`` is the baseline paths minus the allowed paths that the baseline
        actually carries; printing ``allowed_in_unit`` without the in-baseline part leaves
        a reader unable to check ``complement`` without re-running the parser. The two
        counts side by side are what makes the pasted line recomputable on its own.
        """
        reading = compare_working_tree()
        line = reading_line(reading)
        print(line)
        fields = dict(part.split("=", 1) for part in line.split())
        for key in ("compared", "unit", "complement", "allowed_in_unit",
                    "allowed_in_baseline", "frozen_mismatches"):
            assert key in fields, "the reading line carries no %r: %s" % (key, line)
>       assert int(fields["compared"]) == int(fields["unit"]), (
            "the line compares %s of its %s baseline paths — the compared count is not the "
            "unit's own size" % (fields["compared"], fields["unit"]))
